# PGmc disagreement map

GENERATED analytical view; source forms remain separately authoritative.
Reading coverage, analytical review and explanatory certainty are independent.

Core triage compares the recorded citation units and retains every core alternative.
Tags identify reviewed questions; undetermined cases are not settled historical disagreements.
Unknown attribution or date is a review outcome, not source endorsement or a PGmc default.

Citation-unit triage, feature alignment and explanations of divergence have separate accountability.
Position-support reasons do not certify an explanation of inter-author disagreement.

Core cases: 393; different_units: 30; insufficient_evidence: 33; substantive_difference: 2; undetermined: 328.
Core alignment: bounded_limit: 17; unreviewed: 376.
The catalogue below covers every case. Detailed explanations follow only where reasoning is recorded;
the linked source ledger retains the complete alternatives, arguments and verification limits.

| core-1933 / [1933](reconstruction_ledger.md#row-1933) | What do the source units establish for adder (nǣdre)? | consonantism;ending;gender;inflection;lexical_identity;quantity;stem_class;vocalism | undetermined / unestablished | bounded_limit; Feminine long-ē/ōn citations, Orel's separate short-a feminine and masculine/WEUR comparanda are individually distinguished (Orel286; Kroonen381,386). ## Recorded explanations and scoped equivalences

<a id="beaver-colour-direction"></a>

## beaver-colour-direction: Why are animal-to-colour and colour-to-animal derivational directions opposed?

Rows: 1941; reviewed; substantive_difference; types: cognate_grouping;historical_analysis;stem_class; explanation: analyst_inference.
Unit: lexical_identity. Premises: Compare the same beaver/colour question and the authors' stated direction. Kroonen does not name Orel as his interlocutor; relating his objection to Orel's position is CAPR's inference.

| Position | Source / pages | Diplomatic form | Relation / attribution | Analytical interpretation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a-beaver-direction-orel-core-1941-01 | Orel2003 pp.40-41 | *bebruz | same_etymon_citation / endorsed | unspecified (dictionary_context); form: *bebruz; features: {"cell": "masculine headword", "ending": "printed -uz full citation", "gender": "masculine", "vocalism": "written bebr- root; internal consonant group retained"}; Focused comparison of derivational direction, not whole-word historical endpoint equivalence. |
| a-beaver-direction-kroonen-core-1941-1 | Kroonen2013 pp.56-57 | *bebura- | same_etymon_citation / endorsed | unspecified (dictionary_context); form: *bebura-; features: {"cell": "masculine a-stem; homonym1", "gender": "masculine", "stem_class": "a-stem", "vocalism": "written bebur- base"}; Focused comparison of derivational direction, not whole-word historical endpoint equivalence. |

Orel explicitly starts from a colour adjective; Kroonen reverses the usual direction because PIE u-stem colour adjectives were productive and proposes an ablauting animal-name paradigm. This identifies a directional/morphological premise, not a mere spelling normalization or a complete account of Orel's reasons.

- divergence_explanation / morphological_argument (analyst_inference): Orel40-41's adjective-first statement and Kroonen56-57's explicitly reversed animal-first account differ in directional derivation. Kroonen invokes productive PIE u-stem colour adjectives and an ablauting animal-name paradigm; aligning his objection with Orel's position is our bounded inference. [Orel2003 pp.40-41; Kroonen2013 pp.56-57]
  Premises: Both passages concern the same beaver/colour connection; no direct rebuttal of Orel is claimed. Counterarguments/limits: Orel's independent reasons for choosing adjective-first direction and the complete comparative paradigm weighting remain unestablished.

Feminine citations share written long ē and ō; Orel also retains a short-a feminine. Masculine and WEUR antecedents are different units. Fulk's qualified quantity explanation does not settle the extra vocalism or the inter-author cause.

- position_support / morphological_argument (analyst_inference): The quoted feminine and masculine formations belong to separate positions; a shared long-vowel feminine citation does not reconcile short-vowel masculine or alternative feminine forms. [Orel2003 pp.286; Orel2003 pp.286; Orel2003 pp.286; Kroonen2013 pp.386; Kroonen2013 pp.386; Kroonen2013 pp.381]
  Premises: The reason applies only to the stated unit/position; it does not select CAPR's current input. Counterarguments/limits: Explains the comparison boundary, not why every historical alternative arose.

An additional z is not a dental-sign normalization. RT explicitly preserves optional z and a later z-less endpoint; this prevents a false three-author vote and narrows the chronology question without explaining the dictionary divergence.

- position_support / analogy (source_explicit): Recorded argument for this position: The beard sense may develop from prickle/edge; its ablaut slot is judged analogical after the zero grade, supporting possible Germanic borrowing into Balto-Slavic and Latin. [Kroonen2013 pp.54]
  Premises: The reason applies only to the stated unit/position; it does not select CAPR's current input. Counterarguments/limits: This is the position's own stated argument, not an established explanation of the difference between authors. Alternatives, cells and historical stages still require alignment; inherited bibliography proposals are not automatically endorsed.
- descriptive_bridge / chronology (source_explicit): RT180 displays optional z in the PGmc stem and no z in the explicitly PWGmc endpoint; the latter is locally masculine. This is a dated descriptive relation within one account. [RingeTaylor2014 pp.180; RingeTaylor2014 pp.180]
  Premises: The full native optional token is source-proved, not an OCR restoration. It does not identify the undated endpoints intended by Orel and Kroonen. Counterarguments/limits: Whole-cell equivalence, the mechanism of z loss and the dictionaries' loan/cognate explanations are not established by this display.

Written present-root e/i differs between endorsed dictionary citations; Kroonen's rejected i is not agreement and Orel's long-i antecedent is another formation. Ringe explicitly supports short-i through following-j raising. Fulk supplies a j-present argument, not an authored PGmc word; Ringe-Taylor distinguishes underlying/surface and later finite cells with native-text limits. These feature/unit/attribution distinctions are reviewed, but common-endpoint equivalence and the inter-author cause remain unestablished.

  Premises: The source's ask etymon and short-vowel present derivation are distinguished from offer and from long-ī wait. This records a position argument under Ringe's stated conditioner; it does not back-date the intermediates or manufacture a PGmc e-bearing infinitive. Counterarguments/limits: Kroonen's defended e-bearing stem and Orel's long-i antecedent require their own representational and derivational premises. This does not establish why the authors disagree or complete the conditioning census.

Kroonen prints the causative drankjan- and related drink formations; Orel's evidence here is drink/potion/beverage nouns. Family evidence can inform drench, but no Orel causative has been invented. Distinct a/e/u presentations are not a direct conflict between two quotations of the same verb.

- position_support / morphological_argument (source_explicit): Kroonen supplies a causative drench formation; Orel supplies related drink nouns. These source positions explain why the extracted forms have different derivational units. [Kroonen2013 pp.100; Orel2003 pp.74; Orel2003 pp.75]
  Premises: The reason applies only to the stated unit/position; it does not select CAPR's current input. Counterarguments/limits: The family comparison does not supply an authored Orel drench verb.

  Premises: The reason applies only to the stated unit/position; it does not select CAPR's current input. Counterarguments/limits: Identical vowel letters alone do not prove agreement about phonemic inventory.
